Resolved as console lag (not a port bug): seed 732221657 (Garden). The console ran match frame 5301 without any render pass (psDispParticles called 0 times vs the usual 6), which is Melee's own lag behaviour -- simulate, do not draw -- and Dolphin reproduces it deterministically from emulated cycles. With no render the console skipped that frame's particleSort, so its particle lists stayed in insertion order while the port's were re-sorted; the next frame's update walk then handed the same randoms to different generators (three of effect 30977), and rng_draws parted at 5303. Slippi's Dolphin lags the same way on both ends of a netplay match; the port cannot mirror emulated lag frames. The lockstep harness could flag a stop as 'console lag frame' when the console's particle passes for the frame before the divergence are zero.

The two open items-on matrix cells are the same console lag class: Ganon vs Ganon Castle (diverged 2444) has no render pass on console frame 2440, Captain vs Captain Pura (diverged 2476) none on 2472 (psDispParticles counted per frame with MELEE_CODE_BP=803A0088; every neighbouring frame has six). Not port bugs. Harness idea: Core.cpp already prints hf (VI frames) next to mf (match frames) at every breakpoint hit -- a match frame simulated without a VI retrace is a lag frame, and the lockstep could label a stop that follows one as 'console lag frame' instead of a divergence.

Harness: the lockstep now labels these. Dolphin (Core.cpp, the trace hook) sends a "G" line ahead of the row of any sim frame it reached with no VI field since the previous one; pc_lockstep.py prints "console lag frame N" when the first difference is within 6 frames of one, pc_lockstep_matrix.py turns that into the verdict "console-lag", and the random sweep writes the match as "[~]" and goes on instead of stopping. Verified on seed 732221657: verdict console-lag, lag frame 5302, difference 5303. MELEE_LAGDBG=1 (with MELEE_DEBUG_OUT) prints every change in the VI-fields-per-sim-frame delta. What that showed: the console runs an extra sim frame per 1000 VI fields (delta 0 at hf 4520, 5520, 6520, 7520, 8520 -- once every 1001 trace lines), so the "lag frame" is periodic, not a one-off overrun; most of them pass unnoticed and only the one that lands while a particle list has several generators of one effect shows up as a divergence. Whether the same 1001-frame slip happens in Slippi's Dolphin outside the lockstep (in which case the port could mirror it by skipping its particle sort on the same cadence) is not known yet. A worker launched before this change parses the old way and still stops on these; its log carries the label.

Fixed: seed 114995247 (Shrine: Koopa/Marth/Mario/Marth), p3.x at 1200. The CPU's item evaluation (ftCo_800BB220, and ftCo_ItemCheck in ftCo_0A01.c) calls ftCo_800A5980((Fighter*) ip) -- a Fighter routine handed an Item. On the console that reads the word at Item+0x10, the item's kind, and compares it with the motion-id ranges, so item kinds 43..46 are never chased or dodged. On the port the Fighter's motion_id is at a different byte offset (its gobj pointer is eight bytes) and the cast read spawn_kind instead; Marth's level-9 CPU evaluated a kind-43 item the console ignored, predicted it would hit, and air-dodged (routine 7, CpuRetapR) where the console held its course, so the AI hash and then x parted on 1200. Found by: AI dump word #1 (stick) and #6 (routine) differing at 1200 with everything else equal; MELEE_CPUCMDAT / MELEE_CODE_BP=800b463c,800b46b8 showed the two sides writing their commands from different routines; the console's return of ftCo_800BB9B4 (CODE_BP at its return address 800ae57c, r3) was 0 and it never reached lbColl_80006094 while the port did. Note for reading console probes: a CODE_BP line's mf0 is the match frame before the sim it fired in -- the block labelled mf0=N is sim N+1, the same sim as the trace row gframe=N+1. ftCo_800A5980_item() is the PC-side read of ip->kind; both call sites use it.

Fixed: seed 968961189 (Zebes: Marth/Luigi/Young Link/Luigi), p1 and p2 aihash at 6831. Both CPUs' AI dumps differed only in word #18, x54.x -- the closest item's position -- by three ulps: a Maxim Tomato that had bounced off the floor at 6821 came up one ulp fast. Found with hex item positions and velocities added to MELEE_ITEMS on both sides (px/py/vx/vy): inputs to it_8027781C identical, output velocity off. The console's asm (build/GALE01/asm/melee/it/itmaplib.s) computes the bounce's speed as fmuls, fmuls, fadds -- unfused -- and fuses only the dot products with the wall and floor normals; the port's return_sqrt_value reused the fused product_xy. A first attempt fused the XY lengths in lbvector.c instead (the arithmetic happened to match the console's vel.x); the asm says every XY length there is plain, and two matches started on that build parted at frames 14 and 15 (seeds 431137213, 668980937 -- not findings). Lesson: for a fused-op question read the function's asm listing first; the arithmetic can agree by coincidence on one component.

Fixed: seed 496993129 (GreatBay: Jigglypuff/Mewtwo/Mario/Young Link), p1 aihash at 5765 -- the AI's cpu.x568 (top of the hurtbox spread) one ulp high, from Mewtwo's root bone y one ulp low, from his position y one ulp off (inside the trace's position tolerance), from his knockback y 9 ulps off since the DI on exiting hitlag at 5761. The DI (ftCo_8008E5A4) takes atan2f(kb_y, kb_x); the knockback had decayed to zero, and the console's zero was -0.0 where the port's was +0.0. atan2f(-0, x<0) = -pi, atan2f(+0, x<0) = +pi: same direction, angles 2pi apart, so cosf/sinf rounded differently. The -0 came from the knockback decay at 8006B9E4: fnmsubs computes -(a*c - b), which is -0 for an exact-zero result, where the port's fmaf(-a, c, b) gives +0. Now FT_NMSUB(a, c, b) = -fmaf(a, c, -b), exact; the four shared *_NMSUB macros (lbcollision, mplib, grmutecity) are written the same way. Seed 161866934 (GreatBay, AI-hash drift only, left open) is identical to match end since. Other fnmsubs sites written FMA(-a, b, c) remain (about 16, each annotated "fnmsubs"); they differ only in the sign of an exact zero.
